## November 19, 2026 UTC — rotation 208: merchant-profile expiry clock boundary (READ-ONLY)

- **Identity and isolation:** One ChatGPT assistant performed this distinct read-only audit after a separate `json.ts` fix lane. No parallel second reviewer or security certification. Source baseline current-main observation `14ded337a10c140d7a9f9a9238ec038587d925bb`; audit branch `automation/20261119T083600Z/audit/merchant-clock` owns only this ledger entry. Lock metadata observed released at `3ae60de55add2763aa7d7a44033d11b96df13adb`; no integration attempted. Active #262/#264 broad consolidations, recovery #260 and new #265 remain separate.
- **Reproducible selection:** Last ledger blob `a54f3fa7572206040b05cde859b5732860cde438`, prior rotation 207, and three previously non-primary-reviewed export modules sorted `packages/invoice-schema/src/envelope.ts,packages/invoice-schema/src/merchantProfile.ts,packages/invoice-schema/src/sign.ts`. FNV-1a32 over `14ded337a10c140d7a9f9a9238ec038587d925bb|a54f3fa7572206040b05cde859b5732860cde438|rotation208|packages/invoice-schema/src/envelope.ts,packages/invoice-schema/src/merchantProfile.ts,packages/invoice-schema/src/sign.ts` yielded unsigned seed **275333962**, index **1/3**, selecting [`packages/invoice-schema/src/merchantProfile.ts`](https://github.com/djshellshoxxx/GhostPay/blob/14ded337a10c140d7a9f9a9238ec038587d925bb/packages/invoice-schema/src/merchantProfile.ts) blob `a75124bb35d2c319e6936097ef6b4048ac59ec76`. Coverage-first pseudorandom subset, not uniform whole-repository sampling.
- **Trust-boundary review:** `parseShape` (lines 88–133) checks signature fields, merchant-id key binding, array and template limits, HTTPS origins and signed expiry; `verifyMerchantProfile` (156–183) handles strict JSON size, signature verification and the expiry predicate. Followed `apps/web/src/lib/merchantTemplate.ts` (blob `045efb594a73a610662e8f5d45a6b270de0e589e`) lines 56 and 68, which call the verifier with injected `nowSeconds()`, rechecking after asynchronous custody work. Read `packages/invoice-schema/src/merchantProfile.test.ts` (blob `0ee33da251a01df95d7aafae9d3118feb1fd9d82`): valid/tampered signature, origin, profile expiry, input-size, unknown keys, duplicate templates and single-char fuzz coverage. Existing tests inspected; not executed.
- **GP-AUD-208-01 — confirmed source-path correctness gap, conditional exploitability:** `verifyMerchantProfile` does not validate `nowSec`; line **181** only checks `p.exp <= nowSec`. For `Number.NaN`, `undefined` or `-Infinity`, this comparison evaluates false and can return `{ ok: true, profile: p }` for an otherwise valid signed **expired** profile. Independent Node 22 arithmetic reproduction: `1700001000 <= NaN` and `1700001000 <= undefined` both false; the same expiration guard is present in source. The full signed-profile verifier was **not** executed with these clocks; no browser exploit or real-world occurrence is established. Severity **Low** (fail-open expiry if a caller passes an invalid clock), confidence **high** for source logic and **unverified** end-to-end exploitability. No private material or security bypass proven for normal finite `Date.now()` callers.
- **Proposed repair and regression:** Require `Number.isSafeInteger(nowSec)` and a non-negative valid Unix-seconds range, returning a generic `{ok:false,reason:'format'}` or an approved explicit time-rejection reason, before accepting a profile; decide the stable public error policy from existing tests. Add a test using a valid signed profile whose `exp` is in the past and clock values `NaN`, `undefined` coerced through JS, `Infinity`, `-Infinity`, and a fractional clock. Keep valid boundary `now === exp` expired. No change to cryptography, policies or broad integration without a fresh owner claim and test-first repair. Track finding GP-AUD-208-01 until actually regression-tested and independently reviewed; not resolved by this audit.
- **Other findings:** No independently reproduced additional flaw in signed merchant-id derivation, domain separation or structured template/origin limits during bounded inspection. This is **not** a full security audit. No code change, GitHub review approval, main merge, deployment, release, CI gate waiver or real funds occurred in the audit phase. Next rotation prioritize previously unreviewed `envelope.ts` and `sign.ts`, selecting again from current source and current ledger blob.

FNV-1a32 over `dfc75ef2087fa6463ae59e0818abec01796c578d|64202ffe9bf481b24ee1ba89f343908f308cda63|rotation206|packages/invoice-schema/src/envelope.ts,packages/invoice-schema/src/index.ts,packages/invoice-schema/src/json.ts,packages/invoice-schema/src/merchantProfile.ts,packages/invoice-schema/src/passphrase.ts,packages/invoice-schema/src/sign.ts` = unsigned seed **244227820**, index **4/6**, selected [`packages/invoice-schema/src/passphrase.ts`](https://github.com/djshellshoxxx/GhostPay/blob/dfc75ef2087fa6463ae59e0818abec01796c578d/packages/invoice-schema/src/passphrase.ts) blob `3844a9951dcad85559e823cac9abfc6fe8ece3b7`. Selection is deterministic within unreviewed invoice-schema sources, not whole-tree uniform sampling.
- **Read-only source and caller inspection:** `passphrase.ts` checks string/Unicode validity, rejects surrogate errors, applies NFKC, enforces ten code points and 1,024 UTF-8 bytes; only scrypt N=2^15/2^16/2^17 (r=8,p=1), 16-byte salt are accepted before key derivation. Password bytes and AES key are caller-owned and wiped in `envelope.ts` finally blocks; JavaScript strings cannot be zeroized. AAD binds `GPI1-PW\0`, chain ID, compressed signer, KDF params and salt. Inspected [`envelope.ts`](https://github.com/djshellshoxxx/GhostPay/blob/dfc75ef2087fa6463ae59e0818abec01796c578d/packages/invoice-schema/src/envelope.ts) blob `3fef176239da072518b09cb10c372f600935ae44`: 16-KiB decoded fragment cap; parsed KDF allowlist before expensive work; nonce/ciphertext structural checks; one generic wrong-passphrase/tamper error; signed plaintext remains separately verified against embedded signer, and pinning is a distinct trust input. Unauthenticated clear header is explicitly marked untrusted until successful decrypt. [`Pay.tsx`](https://github.com/djshellshoxxx/GhostPay/blob/dfc75ef2087fa6463ae59e0818abec01796c578d/apps/web/src/views/Pay.tsx) blob `579cbe531c39c83745e9a188b176b5a479a32a6b` prompts for a passphrase then uses `openFragment` and stale-flow/session checks; clears password state after success or invoice change.
- **Tests inspected, not executed:** [`passphrase.test.ts`](https://github.com/djshellshoxxx/GhostPay/blob/dfc75ef2087fa6463ae59e0818abec01796c578d/packages/invoice-schema/src/passphrase.test.ts) blob `a80ea26fc2b72a219270418ac2120d3d1f703ef5` contains independent scrypt/AES-GCM vectors, tampered header/nonce/ciphertext comparison, downgrade/substitution refusal, NFKC/surrogate/size cases, invalid KDF pre-derivation, structural fuzzing and typed error checks. No fresh browser, KDF benchmark, microtask cancellation, fault-injection, tests, or independent external security review ran for this audit.
- **Finding/limits:** **No independently reproducible new correctness or security defect established.** Allowed KDF work can be expensive on constrained browsers by design; without a reproducer, do not classify this as a new exploitable denial of service. An encrypted invoice link is neither seller identity attestation nor chain settlement proof; a compromised browser or reused link/passphrase can expose plaintext. The related user-guide information gap is handled by a separate docs PR; this audit modifies **only this ledger**, not product sources or tests. One assistant performed the audit sequentially, **not** a second concurrent worker. No security claim, severity, live-host testing, new dependency or gate change. Next rotation: sample another unreviewed invoice-schema module from the next ledger blob; review callers and tests.

This codec provides byte representation only, **not confidentiality, authentication, signature checking or payer privacy**; those are separate envelope/signature boundaries.

- **Trust boundary / observed checks:** The worker receives one viewing-key buffer and public announcement batches; core parses the compressed spending key, derives the scanning scalar, zeroizes the supplied viewing bytes in a `finally` block, memoizes exact replay checks with a 4096-entry flush bound, emits only public match ID/address/tag and progress, and wipes the retained scalar and replay cache on cancel/end. The main-thread `runScan` owns RPC fetches, receipt verification and checkpoint/reorg decisions. It registers abort and reply-silence timeout handling, terminates the worker, and zeroizes its supplied viewing key. Source-based unit tests check malformed points, cancel/restart/end behavior, key-buffer wiping, replay equivalence and a static prohibition on worker network/storage API imports. The no-network test checks these files' source and imports, **not an isolated browser enforcement of every transitive dependency**.
- **Finding/limits:** No newly proven reproducible correctness/security/privacy flaw established in this inspection. The source evidence does not by itself certify browser memory erasure/JIT/GC, external provider authenticity or runtime worker compromise resistance. No vulnerability severity or speculative public issue filed; no audit-specific test executed. Of 54 direct production `apps/web/src/lib/*.ts` modules (test/spec/worker/testutil suffix excluded), seven had no exact filename mention in the previous audit ledger; sorted index **5/7** selected `apps/web/src/lib/scanSpam.ts` blob `1aaa9a3eb0ca888de74a35e57e71431f419ca6a7`.
- **Source/callers/tests:** Examined `scanSpam.ts:40–69` (router/open lane prioritization without dropping announcements), `:87–114` (sanitized local preferences), and `:116–163` (opt-in same-token amount-frequency estimate). Followed `scanClient.ts` import/caller/telemetry types, `Scan.tsx:245–350` selected RPC, scan result and privacy display, `scanPrivacy.ts` window/query privacy boundary, and `scanSpam.test.ts:181–251` including `mockRpc.ts:9–30`. The code requests `eth_getLogs` with the expected token and window, bounds returned log count and treats provider limit/errors as `unknown`, but `estimateSimilarTransfers` checks only topics/data **when counting**, not each returned log's `address` or `blockNumber`.
- **Finding (conditional, awaiting executed RED):** An RPC that disregards its `eth_getLogs` address or block filter can return a structurally valid, same-amount ERC-20 Transfer from another contract or outside the requested window; by static control flow in `scanSpam.ts:150–157`, this would increase the displayed **advisory same-token transfer count**. The user-chosen RPC is a trust boundary. Source-level reproduction was specified as a test-only [draft PR #159](https://github.com/djshellshoxxx/GhostPay/pull/159), commit `ebbddcb1a5660b5e1f0b9971dd6064e0dfdabdba`, using `mockRpc` to return off-token and out-of-window logs and expecting `unknown:error`. Exact-head CI **not yet established** at audit time; do not claim an executed reproduction or security severity. Impact is an inaccurate optional privacy-set heuristic, **not** proof of an exploitable wallet/payment compromise or a bypass of chain settlement verification. Confidence in source-level gap: high; runtime confirmation pending.

## 2026-11-14 UTC — rotation 93: signed fiat rendering of negative minor units (read-only)

- **Selection:** Owner-controlled Pages [#150](https://github.com/djshellshoxxx/GhostPay/pull/150) pinned at `36bcdb7470caf7e563b4f72cadc209504137fd6f`; prior audit ledger blob `ab2b70ef492ea0bcd87863de684601a1166027a3`. From **34** remaining sorted candidates index **5** selected `apps/web/src/lib/format.ts` (blob `25d2de3ed44dc7dc9d585413c5ac0127c8fe7b05`).
- **Verified low-severity pure formatting correctness defect (not an established payment exploit):** `format.ts:34–39` performs signed bigint division and modulus independently. For negative fractional fiat input, the remainder retains a minus sign and is appended after the decimal point: evaluating the exact source arithmetic for `-5n, 'USD'` produces `USD 0.-5` instead of `USD -0.05`; `-12345n, 'USD'` produces `USD -123.-45` instead of `USD -123.45`. Zero-decimal JPY remains `JPY -2`. Confidence **high** in this pure-helper arithmetic defect; severity **low** because currently signed invoices validate fiat/line-item/tax minor units against unsigned `DEC_RE` (`packages/invoice-schema/src/schema.ts:191–206,270–274,309–313`) and `invoiceCreate.ts:107–126` uses nonnegative parsing. No negative signed-invoice path or authorization bypass was demonstrated. The risk is incorrect financial display if the helper is later called with a negative adjustment/refund.
- **Callers and tests:** Inspected `format.test.ts` for existing positive currency/zero-decimal and units coverage, `views/Pay.tsx:1049–1091` for buyer-facing fiat totals, line items and tax, `views/Sweep.tsx` for independent unsigned token-unit formatting, and `packages/invoice-schema/src/schema.ts` for the trust boundary. Confirmed the outputs using a manual JavaScript evaluation of the same bigint expression; this was **not** an import/run of the repository module or a full test suite. Independent primary development lane created separate [test-first draft #154](https://github.com/djshellshoxxx/GhostPay/pull/154) at `b1150eb8280ac5259fad620f0b762e83c19cf71d` on isolated `chatgpt/zero-codex/pages-negative-fiat-regression-20261114`; regression is intended **RED**, with GitHub CI pending. No production code correction was made; the audit phase itself only inspected source.

- **Concrete probable liveness defect (low severity, high static confidence; CI regression pending):** at `reconciliationVault.ts:69–85`, `dbPromise ??= new Promise(...)` permanently retains a rejected promise if `request.onerror` or `request.onblocked` fires. No catch resets it. Subsequent `read()` or `compareAndSwap()` uses the same rejection even after browser storage becomes available, requiring a reload/new store instance to recover. If an old blocked request later succeeds, its connection is not explicitly closed. This is a storage **availability** concern, not evidence of payment bypass, plaintext storage, fund loss or cryptographic failure. **Reproduction design:** (1) first `indexedDB.open` emits `error` or `blocked`; (2) observe the first read reject; (3) retry with a successful fake IndexedDB open; (4) expect two open attempts, successful null read, and late stale-open closure. Isolated test-first [draft #145](https://github.com/djshellshoxxx/GhostPay/pull/145) `97471cc2f3ec24af705c5227a256a7f20a045952` contains only those regressions. **Do not mark RED verified or the production fix complete before exact-head CI evidence.**

- **Threat boundary and result:** `sessionStorage` deliberately preserves the lock across reloads of **the same tab**, not other tabs; this is an existing documented limitation, not an independently established exploit. A signed direct sweep's exact expected transaction hash is persisted *before* RPC handoff, while a relayed sweep persists its on-chain order identity before posting; malformed/unavailable storage disarms signing in the owning page. Only public owner/nonce/transaction hash/order/status are persisted; destination, amount and private keys are not journal fields. A missing RPC receipt is not treated as proof that broadcasting did not happen. **No new reproducible correctness, security or privacy defect established** by this bounded source review; no severity or proposed fix. - **Source-level low-severity correctness finding (not independently runtime-reproduced):** `packages/ux-guide/src/profiles.ts:227-232` documents rejecting **non-plain-object** questionnaire values, but the check excludes only null, arrays and non-objects. `Object.keys(new Date(0))` is empty; by lines 237-264, such an object would be treated as a valid unanswered questionnaire and return `private`, not `AnswerError`. Likewise `Object.create({ audience: 'adversary', amounts: 'large', waiting: 'hours' })` has no own enumerable keys, but `rec[q]` in lines 242-249 reads inherited values, allowing inherited prototype answers to influence recommendations rather than being rejected. Existing negative tests cover null, arrays, strings, unknown own fields and `constructor`, but not prototype-bearing or Date instances. **Confidence high** for static language/control-flow mismatch; **severity low** for validation/recommendation integrity, **no demonstrated funds, privacy or remote exploit**. Normal JSON-decoded questionnaire objects do not carry custom prototype methods. This was inspection only, not a successful package test or independent security audit.
- **Suggested isolated follow-up:** coordinate a separate owner-approved test-first regression asserting rejection of Date/class-instance/prototype-supplied answers, followed by explicit plain-record and own-property checks while retaining existing scoring/FAQ requirements. Do not change profile safety policy or rely on the recommendation alone as a transaction authorization gate. No code or tests were modified in this read-only audit lane; report issue privately if later investigation establishes a true security impact.

## 2026-11-11 — audit rotation 27: ux-schedule clock-boundary/state-transition consistency (sequential, read-only)

- **Pseudorandom coverage rotation:** pinned unmerged owner combined source [#121](https://github.com/djshellshoxxx/GhostPay/pull/121) at `ca358acfa8f48d4deff9b770214bcba797b6b1f0`, prior audit ledger blob `5631d04a374e3c3f095c0c2b5e058c753ffb3e0f`. Enumerated 69 sorted `packages/` directories, excluded 30 distinct previously referenced packages, leaving 39 unaudited candidates. Unsigned FNV-1a32 on `<source SHA>|<prior audit blob>|<sorted excluded names CSV>` gives `1850933954`, candidate index `38/39`, selecting `packages/ux-schedule/`. Among 8 sorted non-test top-level TypeScript `src/` files, `hash % 8 = 2` selects `src/result.ts` as the starting point. Deterministic varying coverage, not cryptographic randomness.
- **Read-only scope:** inspected `src/result.ts` clock integer bounds and discriminated-result helpers; `time.ts` injected time-zone offset contract; `schedule.ts` evaluation/one-time materialization; `recurrence.ts` bounded wall-clock recurrence; `scheduled.ts` pending/due/confirmed/cancelled/expired transitions, approval and age limits; `validate.ts` object/amount validation; `src/index.ts`, package README, and `schedule.test.ts`, `scheduled.test.ts`, `recurrence.test.ts`. No direct caller beyond package exports was established in repository code search. This is not a payment transaction/signing path: the package explicitly never sends funds.
- **Low-severity correctness observation (source-level control-flow proof, no demonstrated funds or privacy impact):** `scheduled.ts:146-156` first calls `tick(p, clock)`, which reads the clock at `scheduled.ts:126` and allows `now <= p.dueAtMs + p.maxAgeMs`, then calls `readClock(clock)` a **second time** to stamp the `confirmed` state at line 156 without rechecking expiry. For a valid due payment with `dueAtMs=1_000` and `maxAgeMs=60_000`, a clock returning `61_000` followed by `61_001` produces a confirmed state timestamp **after** the expiry boundary, despite the comment at `scheduled.ts:122` saying payments more than `maxAgeMs` overdue can never be confirmed. The existing `scheduled.test.ts:78-83` exercises boundary timestamps using a **fixed** clock, not a two-read boundary crossing. This is a source-derived deterministic example; the GhostPay package tests and an independent integration runtime were **not** executed during this audit. Confidence **high** for double-read inconsistency; severity **low** (confirmation-state bookkeeping, not transfer). `cancel()` similarly obtains two clock values (`scheduled.ts:160-163`), but no security consequence is claimed.
- **Coordination and safe next step:** schedule a separate regression using a clock that crosses the exact max-age boundary between consecutive reads; define whether the approval instant or transition timestamp is authoritative and then use one captured instant or enforce revalidation. Preserve explicit acknowledgment, immutable snapshot checks and no-send behavior. Do not patch the owner #121 source or current #125 CLI branch; notify primary lane after its exact-head CI settles. This is an ordinary robustness/correctness observation, **not a publicly asserted security vulnerability**; handle any independently demonstrated exploit through `SECURITY.md` private reporting.
- **Limits:** source and existing test **inspection only**; no Vitest/Node package tests, chain, wallet, real network, security certification or cryptographic assessment. ## 2026-11-11 — audit rotation 26: scan-source public-log privacy claims and structural validation (sequential, read-only)

- **Deterministic rotation:** source is still owner combined draft [#121](https://github.com/djshellshoxxx/GhostPay/pull/121) at `ca358acfa8f48d4deff9b770214bcba797b6b1f0`. Of 69 sorted first-party `packages/` directories, excluded 29 previously referenced review directories; unsigned FNV-1a32 of `<source SHA>|<prior ledger blob>|<sorted excluded package names CSV>` yielded `923144544`, eligible index `24/40`, choosing previously unaudited `packages/scan-source/`. Non-test top-level `src/*.ts` list had 14 entries; index `0/14` selected **`src/claims.ts`**. This is deterministic coverage rotation, not cryptographically random selection.
- **Read-only trace:** inspected `src/claims.ts` / `types.ts` UI disclosure strings and network-profile assumptions; `create.ts` / `guard.ts` mainnet refusal and mode selection; `range.ts` injected backend, bounded scans and local matching; `indexer.ts` headers, second-source cross-checks, bounded response and padded query; `pir2.ts` two-server noncollusion, third-source header and epoch policy; `light.ts` asserted header chain/log-inclusion distinction; `quorum.ts` all-fulfilled-source agreement and intersected coverage; `validate.ts` raw record normalization; `window.ts` padding. Also inspected `validate.test.ts`, `indexer.test.ts`, `range.test.ts`, `pir2.test.ts`, `composite.test.ts`, `window.test.ts`, `src/index.ts` and package README. No active application scan caller or real RPC/PIR/Tor/network runtime was validated in this phase.
- **Concrete low-severity correctness observation, no demonstrated remote exploit:** `src/validate.ts:29-42` promises that `parseAnnouncement(raw: unknown)` “never throws on hostile input,” yet `r['schemeId']` at line 35 and later property reads at lines 36-42 directly invoke JavaScript accessors. A hostile *in-process adapter object* with `Object.defineProperty({}, 'schemeId', {get() { throw Error('injected getter'); }})` throws from the getter instead of returning `undefined`. Node v22.16.0 executed an equivalent property-read reproduction, logging one getter invocation and the thrown error. The package function itself and its Vitest suite were **not executed**; the exact source access is unguarded, making the source-level discrepancy high confidence. `RangeBackend.getAnnouncements` returns `unknown[]` and `range.ts:fetch` passes each item directly to this validator; an in-process untrusted/malicious backend object could abort a scan rather than be counted as malformed. Ordinary network JSON decoding cannot contain accessors, so **no remote network reachability, private-data disclosure or payment compromise is established**. Impact is robustness of the untrusted-object contract; severity **low (correctness)**.
- **Recommended non-overlapping follow-up:** after the primary #125 CI/task finishes and ownership is coordinated, add an isolated regression with getter/proxy throwing on each input field, expect the documented nonthrowing/skip contract, and apply minimal fail-closed handling without echoing exception text. Also test that no partial result is promoted. This is a source-level finding for the primary owner, **not** an independently audited security vulnerability or a public advisory. Do not modify the integration owner's `#121` source or financial/crypto protocol to address it.
- **Privacy and release limits:** `claims.ts` correctly qualifies RPC/IP/range leakage, third-party trust and PIR noncollusion; light-client block-header confirmation is not log-completeness proof unless the external client really verifies logs. All modes remain testnet-only, and this review is not independent privacy/cryptographic certification. No local package/Vitest/Forge/real-network tests, exploit, external keys, second concurrent audit worker, or paid API agents were used. One ChatGPT assistant performed this distinct **sequential read-only audit phase** and changed only this ledger on isolated [draft #103](https://github.com/djshellshoxxx/GhostPay/pull/103). ## 2026-11-11 — audit rotation 22: onion URL canonical-input boundary (sequential source review)

- **Deterministic source selection:** owner combined draft [#121](https://github.com/djshellshoxxx/GhostPay/pull/121) at `ca358acfa8f48d4deff9b770214bcba797b6b1f0`, unchanged since checkpoint 21. Previous audit blob `528d731d0e226789bfbda6540e85b5220c730991`. Enumerated 69 sorted `packages/` directories, excluded the 25 names mentioned in prior durable source-review entries (including related caller packages), leaving 44 previously unaudited candidates. Unsigned FNV-1a32 over `<source SHA>|<prior audit blob>|<sorted excluded names CSV>` produced **4222356045**, index **17/44** selected `packages/net-transport/`; index **12/21** among sorted non-test top-level TypeScript files selected `src/onion.ts`.
- **Read-only inspection:** `packages/net-transport/src/onion.ts:30-87`, `src/core.test.ts:206-270`, `src/profiles.ts` (the onion profile's `validateOnionUrl` + `parseTargetUrl` caller and SOCKS5h transport), `src/url.ts`, `src/proxyUrl.ts`, `src/socks5.ts`, `src/httpConnect.ts`, `src/credentials.ts`, `src/guard.ts`, `src/settings.ts`, `src/profile.ts`, `src/claims.ts`, `src/socks.test.ts`, package README, and `docs/remote/privacy-specs/wiring/net-transport.md`. Verified source-level boundaries for testnet restriction, remote proxy DNS, userinfo and fragments, HTTP only on onion opt-in, secret-free errors, SOCKS credential isolation, and explicit absence of live Tor/OHTTP qualification. No production changes, independent audit, real network use or installed project test run.
- **Confirmed low-impact canonicalization discrepancy (correctness, not an established exploit):** `validateOnionUrl` documents rejection of noncanonical RAW authorities (lines 59-62), but its raw authority check (lines 73-76) only rejects uppercase/userinfo before `new URL(raw)` (line 80) percent-decodes the host. `isValidV3OnionHost(u.hostname)` at line 85 sees the normalized canonical hostname. Consequently a `%64`-encoded leading letter or `%2e`-encoded dot in an otherwise valid lowercase v3 onion authority passes and returns the canonical URL. A standalone Node 22 reproduction of these exact normalization/validation steps with the existing publicly documented DuckDuckGo v3 test host returned the same canonical URL for canonical input, `%64`-encoded first character and `%2e`-encoded dot; no external network access, secrets, or transactions were used. Existing `core.test.ts` checks uppercase, typos and userinfo but not percent-encoded authority. **Impact:** the parser accepts noncanonical raw spelling contrary to its documented NP-T-7 contract; it still validates the normalized v3 onion checksum, and **no hostname/authentication bypass, information leak, or security exploit is established**. Confidence high for the inspected logic and Node URL parser reproduction; severity **low (input-canonicalization correctness)**, not a security advisory.
- **Proposed test-first next action, not performed:** primary owner should verify this contract is intentionally strict and, on a separately owned nonoverlapping test-only branch, add negative regression cases for percent-encoded authority; then apply a minimal raw-authority validation fix without changing the normalized valid-host behavior. Coordinate with owner #121 first. Do not silently broaden protocol assumptions or make an unreviewed security claim. No GitHub public security issue/discussion was opened; no regression PR is claimed created.
- **Concurrency and verification:** separate audit phase performed sequentially by one ChatGPT assistant; no second concurrent worker or paid agent was available. Local Node reproduced only the parser boundary, **not** full package tests, proxy/network behavior, live Tor authentication or cryptographic acceptance. Audit ledger update is on isolated [draft #103](https://github.com/djshellshoxxx/GhostPay/pull/103); this commit requires fresh exact-head scoped CI before qualification.

- **Read-only source review:** `packages/ux-intake/src/qr.ts` (frame grammar, per-frame/aggregate caps, 16-frame ceiling, mismatch/replay/conflict and 60s assembly TTL, camera-permission view-model), neighboring `src/payload.ts` (untrusted URL, exact host/route and fragment parsing, 16 KiB underlying envelope limit), `src/deeplink.ts` (redirect allowlists, share target, log redaction), `src/address.ts` (EVM syntax vs unverified EIP-55, Bech32 checksum), `src/nlamount.ts` (allowlisted exact-decimal amounts), `src/result.ts` and `src/index.ts`; inspected source assertions in `qr.test.ts`, `payload.test.ts`, `deeplink.test.ts`, `address.test.ts`, package README and proposed `docs/remote/usability/wiring/ux-intake.md`. No executable source tests, web UI/QR decoder, browser, signing, or network checks performed in this audit.
- **Trust boundaries and concrete observations:** QR frames are unauthenticated and represent only staged strings; completed invoices require later `parseFragment` and independent verification, not merely `status: complete`. The parser rejects malformed frame indexes, new transfer IDs during assembly, contradictory chunks, nested frames, forbidden schemes/hosts, userinfo/query on invoice links, oversized reassembly and non-ASCII/control characters; a retained frame state is explicitly caller-controlled and nonpersistent. `safeDisplay` hides invoice bearer fragments while `payload.fragment` intentionally retains them for downstream processing; logging raw `Payload` would still leak secrets. `parseShareTarget` warns when a bearer fragment has entered a query; preventing an HTTP server from receiving that query requires the proposed service-worker/no-network routing and browser checks, which the wiring document does not establish as implemented. Mixed-case EVM checksum is flagged *unverified*, so syntax classification must never be treated as recipient authentication. Existing tests assert representative negative cases and caps, but were not executed here.

No live UI wiring, decrypted/signed-invoice authentication, production privacy guarantee, crypto audit or external service-worker behavior is inferred. The client compares both manifests and SHA-256 of reconstructed pages against those untrusted agreed manifest leaves; optional third-source block hash provides a separate head-context check, but does **not by itself establish signed manifest publisher identity or independent source authentication**. Dummy query scheduling limits index disclosure in single-server views, but cannot hide client IP, query timing or server collusion; the user-facing `PIR2_CLAIMS` explicitly says so.
- **Verification and finding:** inspected existing tests for pair rejection, uniform query behavior, fixed-size replies, malicious response/page mismatch, testnet gating, source hash mismatch and no-privacy messaging **by source only**. - **Trust model:** experimental **testnet-only** delayed forwarding, explicitly described as weak and **not unlinkable**. Address allocator, key-scoped accessor, RPC reads, relayer quotes and submits, clock, random source and optional sealing are caller-injected. No untrusted-plan injection exploit or security bypass was reproduced, so this is a trust-boundary review item **without a severity/vulnerability claim**. Package docs already disclose optional sealing and incomplete deployment/Anvil acceptance. Continue follow-up only with exact caller provenance and a reproducible negative test.
- **Finding/evidence:** **no new demonstrated or reproduced exploitable defect** in this source-only inspection. No assertions, fuzzing, on-chain e2e, runtime tests or independent security audit executed; no secrets accessed. The separate operator-monitor bounded-network-fetch fix in draft #106 belongs to the primary development lane, not this audit branch.

- **Files inspected:** `packages/secret-box/src/secretBox.ts`, `scope.ts`, `index.ts` references via exports, `secretBox.test.ts`, `scope.test.ts`, `secret-box.killers.test.ts`, package README and DEVIATIONS. Consumer/trust boundary: `apps/web/src/lib/session.ts` and `session.test.ts`.
- **Trust boundaries:** secret buffers and lifecycle, synchronous/async temporary borrowing, best-effort zeroization, one-way transfer, serialization/logging guardrails, structural preflight of outgoing payloads, seller-key storage and session invalidation. Browser-compromise limitations are explicitly acknowledged in source comments.
- **Specific observations:** `SecretBox.copyOf` defaults to source wiping; `use/useAsync` wipe borrowed views on callback exit; `take` transfers ownership without wiping; `SecretScope.close` wipes tracked buffers and boxes and is idempotent; `assertNoSecretBox` rejects secret boxes, excessive/opaque structures and accessor properties. The caller in `apps/web/src/lib/session.ts` borrows ephemeral scalar copies and supplies separate async entry points. Existing tests address serialization, object reflection, async cleanup, transfer, cycles, malicious array methods, wiping and session changes.
- **Findings:** **no new reproducible defect established** by source inspection. The audit does not certify these flows safe or independently validate JIT/GC wiping, browser Worker/IndexedDB behavior, or all callers. No speculative vulnerability filed, no sensitive exploit details disclosed.

Coordinate with current owners before a fix PR. No change to production code or security gates.
